PRD_SRS.md — 197 lines
# AIVX — Product Requirements and Software Requirements Specification

**Document ID:** AIVX-PRD-001 / AIVX-SRS-001  
**Version:** 1.0 · **Date:** 2026-11-26  
**Status:** PRE-BUILD BASELINE — scope largely accepted; detailed prioritization remains OPEN  
**Audience:** user, Android Build agent, backend architect, QA  
**Related:** FEAT-01..15, FR-001..090, NFR-001..045, SCR-01..14, DATA-001..030, API-001..045, SEC-001..030, TEST-001..080

## 1. Product vision

A private online video creation and professional editing workspace on Android. The user can create or import footage, ask an AI to analyze/reference-reconstruct or generate video content, receive a truly editable timeline where output elements are represented as layers when possible, manually refine every supported property, select exact visual elements with on-frame gestures, collect multiple comments, and execute all approved changes together through a verified AI editing pipeline.

It is not a one-shot generator whose output cannot be changed. It is also not a promise to reconstruct hidden source files, proprietary fonts, 3D geometry or exact animation from a flattened MP4. Reconstruction quality depends on available source/project data, visual evidence, provider model capability, and whether the original element is editable or rasterized.

## 2. User and environment

- Primary role: owner/editor.
- Additional roles: invited friend/editor/commenter/viewer — final permissions OPEN.
- Target platform: Android phones; tablet adaptation is desirable but not yet prioritized.
- Network assumption: app requires internet to sign in, access projects, upload, request AI work, preview cloud jobs and export. Clear connectivity errors are required.
- Device: Android version/minimum API level OPEN; screen sizes and GPU capability vary.
- App distribution: private APK/AAB path; public Play Store launch N/A unless requested.

## 3. Scope

### In scope

- Native Android project UI and navigation.
- Cloud-authenticated private workspace and projects.
- Media upload/import to cloud, proxy and metadata generation.
- Canonical editable timeline/layers and project persistence.
- Natural-language generation and edit planning.
- Visual region/point/time annotation and batch comment execution.
- Manual editor tools for approved timeline/effect capabilities.
- Provider profile management for user-supplied APIs.
- Background jobs with progress, retry/cancel and QC.
- Project export, supported interchange adapters and limitation reports.
- Security, access control, retention and private sharing with friends.

### Out of scope unless later approved

- Public social network, public creator profiles, monetization, ads, SEO, marketing and public launch.
- Local/offline AI or local editing engines on the phone.
- Full desktop editor replacement, desktop app or cross-platform app suite.
- Silent arbitrary shell execution by the AI.
- Universal import compatibility for every third-party editor without a format adapter and round-trip tests.

## 4. User journeys

### Journey A — create from prompt
1. User creates a project and chooses aspect ratio/resolution/frame rate.
2. User describes intended video; may attach reference media.
3. AI proposes brief, style plan, shots, asset plan, timeline structure and expected job/cost.
4. User reviews/approves the plan and any paid generation operation.
5. Cloud jobs generate/find assets and build editable sequence/layers.
6. User previews and edits manually or with AI.
7. User exports or asks for a supported interchange format.

### Journey B — analyze and reconstruct an existing video
1. User uploads video.
2. Probe service stores metadata and creates review proxy.
3. Scene detector creates cuts; ASR provides timestamped words; OCR identifies on-screen text; VLM describes shots/actions/camera/layout; audio model identifies speech/music/SFX and timing; tracking/segmentation may identify objects/layers.
4. A timeline-oriented report separates `OBSERVED`, `INFERRED`, `UNKNOWN` and `LOW CONFIDENCE` facts.
5. Reconstruction planner proposes editable text/shape/vector/2.5D/3D elements where evidence supports it and video/raster proxies where not.
6. User approves; worker builds a draft project and renders review frames.
7. User visually reviews, adds comments, and reruns selected parts.

### Journey C — select one visual target and request a change
1. User pauses at a frame or selects a time range.
2. User circles/lassos/points to a visible object.
3. Client writes normalized geometry in display space and captures current sequence timestamp and project revision.
4. Backend creates target context by intersecting geometry/time with layers, OCR/text objects, masks, clip IDs and assets.
5. User enters a short request such as “change this font”.
6. The agent resolves the target to the editable text entity or marks the item raster-only/ambiguous.
7. It stores a structured comment and shows target chips (time, target, layer, confidence).

### Journey D — collect many comments and execute together
1. User watches and adds comments at 2s, 5s, 8s, etc.
2. Comments stay independent, each with their own revision/context and target references.
3. User selects Run.
4. Planner groups compatible changes, detects conflicts, checks project revision and computes operation ordering/dependencies.
5. User sees a dry-run list, affected layers/times, preview thumbnails, confidence, estimated cost and any destructive/raster fallback steps.
6. User approves all or a subset.
7. System applies the batch as one atomic undoable action, then renders validation previews/QC.
8. User accepts, adjusts, or undoes the batch.

### Journey E — BYOK provider management
1. User creates provider profile and chooses protocol (OpenAI-style, Anthropic-style, custom adapter).
2. User enters endpoint, model identifier, secret and supported capabilities.
3. Server validates endpoint and runs a minimal non-destructive connection test.
4. Profile lists supported text/image/audio/video/function-calling/streaming/context features and pricing configuration.
5. AI router selects only compatible profiles; user can set priority/fallback manually.

## 5. Feature catalogue

| Feature ID | Feature | Baseline status | Main dependencies |
|---|---|---|---|
| FEAT-01 | NLE Timeline & Core Editing | APPROVED SCOPE; implementation OPEN | Canonical model, render engine |
| FEAT-02 | Keyframe & Motion Curve Editor | APPROVED SCOPE; implementation OPEN | Timeline, timebase |
| FEAT-03 | Layering & Compositing | APPROVED SCOPE; 2D baseline first | Render graph |
| FEAT-04 | 3D Scene / Camera / Assets | APPROVED SCOPE; phased/worker-based | Blender or other 3D worker, interchange |
| FEAT-05 | Vector, Drawing & Motion Graphics | APPROVED SCOPE | SVG/shape model, code renderer |
| FEAT-06 | Masks, Tracking, Rotoscoping | APPROVED SCOPE; optional workers | SAM2, RAFT, Track-Anything |
| FEAT-07 | Multitrack Audio Studio | APPROVED SCOPE | Audio graph/DSP, ASR/music workers |
| FEAT-08 | Typography & Text Animation | APPROVED SCOPE | Text shaping/font metrics, layer model |
| FEAT-09 | Color Management & Grading | APPROVED SCOPE | OCIO/LUT/color pipeline |
| FEAT-10 | VFX & Filters | APPROVED SCOPE; catalog phased | Render graph, OpenFX-style contracts |
| FEAT-11 | Stabilization & Restoration | APPROVED SCOPE; job based | Gyroflow/RAFT/RIFE/SR models |
| FEAT-12 | Export & Interchange | APPROVED SCOPE; format-by-format validation | FFmpeg/OTIO/FilmCraft adapters |
| FEAT-13 | AI Creation & Editing Assistant | APPROVED SCOPE | Provider gateway, agent tools |
| FEAT-14 | Video Reverse Engineering | APPROVED SCOPE | Probe, scene, OCR, ASR, VLM, tracking |
| FEAT-15 | Visual Feedback & Batch Revision | APPROVED SCOPE; core differentiator | Annotation anchors, comments, planner, atomic command batch |

## 6. Functional requirements (FR)

### Project and media
- FR-001: User can create, rename, duplicate and delete private projects. Delete requires confirmation and retention policy execution.
- FR-002: User can upload supported video/audio/image assets with resumable progress, cancellation and checksum verification.
- FR-003: System probes each asset and stores codec, container, resolution, frame rate, VFR/CFR, duration, timebase, rotation, color metadata, audio streams and file checksum.
- FR-004: System creates a proxy/thumbnail/waveform where permitted and links every derivative to the original asset.
- FR-005: Missing/unavailable media produces an explicit offline state, not silent blank output.
- FR-006: Project saves use revision numbers and schema versions; older schemas migrate deterministically; newer unsupported schema is refused rather than destructively rewritten.

### Timeline and edit
- FR-010: User can create sequences with explicit canvas size, aspect ratio, frame rate, audio rate and color settings.
- FR-011: User can add video, audio, image, text, shape, vector, adjustment, mask and supported generated layers.
- FR-012: User can trim, split, move, ripple delete, duplicate, reorder, group, lock, hide, mute and link clips.
- FR-013: User can set crop, anchor, position, scale, rotation, opacity, blend mode, playback speed, reverse and freeze frame where engine supports it.
- FR-014: User can keyframe supported clip/effect parameters with linear, hold, Bezier and approved easing curves.
- FR-015: Timeline snapping, markers, guides, safe areas and frame-accurate seek are supported.
- FR-016: Every successful edit is undoable/redoable and associated with an operation ID.
- FR-017: Invalid ranges, overlaps, invalid keyframe values and unsupported operation combinations are rejected with actionable messages.

### Visual feedback / comments
- FR-020: User can draw a circle, freehand lasso, rectangle, point marker, arrow or time-range selection on the preview.
- FR-021: Gesture coordinates are transformed from screen coordinates to normalized stage coordinates and preserve preview scaling/letterboxing offsets.
- FR-022: The annotation records sequence time and frame index/timebase, source-media time where applicable, selected range, project revision, selected object IDs and geometry.
- FR-023: The server resolves candidate editable entities by time intersection, bounding boxes/masks and semantic recognition; the agent must report ambiguity.
- FR-024: User can attach text instruction and optional priority to a comment.
- FR-025: Comments remain separately stored and can be edited, resolved, ignored, reordered or grouped.
- FR-026: Run creates a batch proposal; no edit is applied until the user approves.
- FR-027: Planner reports conflicts, missing layers, stale project revision and raster-only targets before execution.
- FR-028: Approved batch is atomic and undoable as one group; failure rolls back the project mutation.
- FR-029: Post-batch verification inspects the changed timestamps/regions and returns PASS/FAIL/NEEDS REVIEW per comment.

### AI/provider
- FR-030: User can create/edit/disable/delete provider profiles.
- FR-031: Secret is submitted directly to the secure server endpoint and thereafter represented by a secret reference, never echoed to the client.
- FR-032: Provider adapter declares supported modalities/features and a connection test result.
- FR-033: AI output is an `EditPlan` with validated typed operations, target IDs, time ranges, confidence, evidence and unresolved questions.
- FR-034: Agent only uses allowlisted tools with scoped project/job permissions.
- FR-035: Paid or costly AI/generation/GPU tasks show estimated spend and require policy-defined approval.

### Analysis and generation
- FR-040: Reverse analysis produces timestamped shot records and evidence labels OBSERVED/INFERRED/UNKNOWN.
- FR-041: Scene/shot boundaries may be proposed by scene detection and must be reviewable.
- FR-042: Transcript supports word-level start/end, confidence, speaker and language where model provides it.
- FR-043: OCR stores text box geometry, timestamps and confidence.
- FR-044: VLM can describe visual events, framing, objects, camera motion and text but must preserve uncertainty.
- FR-045: Generated media is stored as an asset with model/provider/prompt/seed/parameters/task ID and editable provenance where exposed.
- FR-046: Generation task retries are bounded and deduplicated where provider supports task IDs.

### Export and collaboration
- FR-050: Export settings include format/container, codec, resolution, frame rate, quality/bitrate, audio settings, color and alpha where supported.
- FR-051: Export job includes progress, cancellation and final technical report.
- FR-052: Interchange exports include a feature-loss report and list unsupported objects/effects.
- FR-053: User can share a project with invited friends using role-scoped access; public link is off by default.

## 7. Non-functional requirements (NFR)

- NFR-001 Security: all API endpoints require authentication except health/readiness endpoints with no private data.
- NFR-002 Authorization: project, asset, provider and job access is checked server-side on every operation.
- NFR-003 Secrets: external provider keys never ship in APK, database plaintext, logs or analytics.
- NFR-004 Privacy: media is private by default; uploads disclose which AI provider receives which media and for what task.
- NFR-005 Reliability: uploads and long jobs resume after process/app restart where supported.
- NFR-006 Consistency: command application is atomic; revision conflicts do not silently overwrite changes.
- NFR-007 Determinism: same project revision + same settings + same engine/version should produce reproducible frame output within documented codec/GPU tolerance.
- NFR-008 Performance: interaction commands return quickly; expensive operations are asynchronous jobs with progress.
- NFR-009 Scalability: workers have per-job CPU/RAM/GPU/time quotas and queue limits.
- NFR-010 Accessibility: controls have labels, touch targets, keyboard/assistive navigation and contrast review.
- NFR-011 Observability: logs redact secrets/media contents and expose job IDs, timing, engine versions and failure reason.
- NFR-012 Recovery: snapshots, backups, restore tests and retention deletion are specified before friend sharing.
- NFR-013 Cost: configurable monthly/per-job ceilings and job-level estimated/actual cost.
- NFR-014 Compatibility: supported Android versions/devices and codecs are published in-app.
- NFR-015 Graceful degradation: unavailable provider or worker never destroys editable project state.

## 8. Scope staging — proposed priority

**P0 (architecture/build spike):** Native project shell, identity choice, cloud API contract, project revision/command shell, asset upload/probe, one preview, one basic clip edit, one render job.  
**P1 (editing foundation):** tracks/clips, transform/crop, trims/splits, undo/redo, text layers, captions/audio, autosave/recovery.  
**P2 (AI workflow):** provider gateway, transcript/VLM analysis, prompt plan, FEAT-15 comment collection and atomic batch.  
**P3 (advanced):** masks/tracking, color, VFX, 3D, stabilization/restoration, advanced audio and more exporters.  
This staging is a PROPOSAL; user requested high scope but approved implementation order is not yet established beyond docs-first.

## 9. Acceptance and traceability

Every FR must link to one FEAT, one or more SCR screens, DATA/API entities, SEC requirements and TEST cases. See `TRACEABILITY.md`. Every item remains `PLANNED` until implementation evidence is recorded. No repository README claim makes an FR PASS.
ARCHITECTURE.md — 147 lines
# AIVX — System Architecture and Technical Design

**Document ID:** AIVX-ARCH-001  
**Version:** 1.0 · **Date:** 2026-11-26  
**Status:** PROPOSAL — not yet approved as a deployable cloud design  
**Phase:** D — Technical Design  
**Related IDs:** ADR-001..ADR-012, FEAT-01..FEAT-15, FR-001..FR-090, NFR-001..NFR-045, DATA-001..DATA-030, API-001..API-045, SEC-001..SEC-030, TEST-001..TEST-080, RISK-001..RISK-040

## 1. Purpose and non-negotiable constraints

AIVX is a private, online-only, native Android video-editing application with AI-assisted generation, reverse engineering and editing. The Android app is the user-facing editor surface. The authoritative project state and all expensive media processing run on authenticated cloud services. The Android APK must not contain privileged provider keys, cloud service-account credentials, FFmpeg binaries, CUDA runtimes, model checkpoints, or a second shadow copy of canonical project state.

**APPROVED:** native Android; Kotlin + Jetpack Compose preferred; online-only workflow; user-provided provider credentials; prompt-based editing; visual circle/point/range comments; batch execution after reviewing all comments.  
**PROPOSAL:** cloud control plane + canonical project API + object storage + job workers + provider gateway + rendering services.  
**OPEN:** cloud vendor, region, database, object store, billing ceiling, GPU availability, authentication method, data retention, and final NLE engine.

## 2. Logical architecture

```mermaid
flowchart TB
  A[Native Android Client<br/>Kotlin + Compose] -->|Auth/session| B[API Gateway]
  A -->|Resumable upload| C[Object Storage]
  A -->|Project commands / comments| B
  A -->|Preview stream / job events| B
  B --> D[Identity & Authorization]
  B --> E[Project Service]
  B --> F[Feedback & Annotation Service]
  B --> G[AI Orchestrator]
  B --> H[Job Scheduler]
  G --> I[Provider Gateway / BYOK Vault]
  G --> J[Video Understanding Workers]
  G --> K[Creative / Generation Workers]
  H --> L[Media Processing Workers]
  H --> M[Render Workers]
  L --> N[FFmpeg / Codec Toolchain]
  M --> O[FilmCraft Candidate Engine]
  M --> P[Specialist Renderers: HyperFrames / Remotion / Blender / Natron]
  E --> Q[(Canonical Project Database)]
  F --> Q
  E --> C
  J --> C
  L --> C
  M --> C
  I --> R[External User-Configured AI APIs]
```

## 3. Major services and ownership

### 3.1 Android Client — native only

Responsibilities: sign-in UI; private workspace/project navigation; upload queue; media bin; playback/preview; timeline interaction; layer inspector; drawing annotation tools; comment list; natural-language prompt editor; AI proposal diff; batch approval; render queue; export download; offline/error UI; accessibility and device adaptation.

The client sends commands and renders server-provided project snapshots. It may keep a cache for responsiveness, but every edit must be reconciled against server version and command response. Cached state is never authoritative. Android-specific media APIs may be used for playback and platform UI, but not as a substitute for the cloud renderer or canonical editor model.

### 3.2 API Gateway / Control Plane

Responsibilities: authenticated API routing; request IDs; rate limiting; payload validation; idempotency; API versioning; authorization checks; job creation/status/cancellation; signed upload/download URLs; safe error mapping; audit events. Public APIs must never expose arbitrary filesystem paths or shell commands.

### 3.3 Identity and authorization

The private app still needs proper identity. An account may belong to the owner or an invited friend. Project roles are minimal: `OWNER`, `EDITOR`, `COMMENTER`, `VIEWER`. Role capabilities are enforced server-side. Invitation flow and account recovery remain OPEN. No project or media is public by default.

### 3.4 Project Service

Owns the canonical, versioned project document and its command journal. Validates commands, checks lock/version conflicts, applies one atomic command or batch, records before/after revisions, supports undo/redo through inverse commands or snapshots, emits change events, and creates immutable render snapshots. It rejects invalid media ranges, unknown layer IDs, invalid keyframes, out-of-bounds masks and unsupported engine features.

### 3.5 Feedback / Annotation Service

Stores visual comment anchors with sequence time, source frame/time, normalized coordinates, object/track/layer/clip/asset references, selected region geometry, text OCR/vision hints, capture metadata and project revision. On every comment write it records the exact project revision and a compact context snapshot so that later timeline edits cannot silently change what the comment refers to.

### 3.6 AI Orchestrator

Turns prompts and saved comments into structured, typed edit plans. It selects tools based on capability manifests; asks for missing details only where necessary; retrieves relevant frames/audio/transcripts; invokes VLM/ASR/OCR/object segmentation workers; converts user intent to operations; validates them against project schemas; estimates uncertainty/cost; prepares dry-run changes; asks for approval; executes an atomic batch; verifies the actual render; and produces a report. The LLM never directly writes arbitrary project JSON or executes arbitrary code.

### 3.7 Provider Gateway / BYOK vault

Stores user-configured API profiles encrypted server-side. It normalizes protocol differences only where an adapter exists. Every provider adapter declares: endpoint base URL; auth type; secret reference; required headers; request schema; response parser; streaming support; model ID; supported input types; video/audio/image context limits; maximum file size; timeout; retry policy; region; retention notes; estimated token/frame pricing; rate limits; error mapping. A custom adapter must be explicitly configured and tested. “OpenAI-compatible” is a protocol family, not proof that every custom model implements every feature.

### 3.8 Storage

Object storage contains source media, derived proxies, thumbnails, waveforms, extracted frames, OCR/transcripts, mask sequences, render caches, exports, model reports, and snapshots. Project metadata in the database points to versioned object IDs, never raw user-supplied URLs as trusted paths. Use signed, short-lived scoped URLs for uploads/downloads. Lifecycle deletion must account for versions, derived objects and backups.

### 3.9 Job scheduler and workers

Long jobs are asynchronous and checkpointed. Worker classes: `MEDIA_PROBE`, `PROXY`, `FRAME_EXTRACT`, `SCENE_DETECT`, `ASR`, `OCR`, `VLM_ANALYSIS`, `TRACKING`, `SEGMENTATION`, `INPAINT`, `UPSCALE`, `INTERPOLATION`, `STABILIZATION`, `3D_RENDER`, `COMPOSITE`, `FINAL_RENDER`, `QC`, `EXPORT_ADAPTER`. Jobs need queue limits, cancellation, retry bounds, idempotency, resource class, max duration, cost ceiling and isolated temporary workspace.

## 4. Canonical editing engine strategy

**Primary candidate:** `storytold/filmcraft` headless Rust NLE. Its architecture documents an engine-first session/command model, exact integer ticks and rational frame rates, a CPU-reference/GPU-fast-path renderer, project schema versioning, undo, scopes, codecs and multiple interchange formats. Its current repository README/docs are evidence of design, not proof that it compiles on our cloud target or fits our data model. `filmcraft-desktop/filmcraft` has a similar architecture tree and should not be assumed identical to `storytold/filmcraft`; the latter has the more extensive architecture/testing/project-files/interchange documentation fetched for this review.

**Gate before adoption:** pin commit; inspect full source and licence/configs; build server-side on intended Linux image; run `cargo xtask ci`; validate codecs; test headless project creation/import/trim/keyframe/mask/audio/mix/preview/render/cancel/undo/redo/persistence/reopen; benchmark 1080p and 4K; check RAM/GPU use; validate concurrent sessions; prove deterministic operation; create an adapter to AIVX canonical schema or formally select FilmCraft `.fcproj` as canonical. No decision to replace the app-wide schema with FilmCraft has been made.

**Media foundation:** FFmpeg/ffprobe are the codec, probing, decode/encode and filter tools. They are not the project model or user experience. A media worker must validate all input paths, stream metadata, durations, timestamps, color metadata, audio tracks, VFR/CFR and resource requirements before processing.

**Specialist tools:** Remotion/HyperFrames for deterministic code-generated motion; Blender for 3D; Natron/OpenFX for specialist compositing; SAM2/Track-Anything/RAFT for segmentation/tracking/flow; ProPainter for inpainting; RIFE for frame interpolation; Gyroflow for stabilization; Real-ESRGAN/GFPGAN/waifu2x for optional restoration. Each remains an isolated worker behind typed API contracts. Tool output is flattened or translated into editable layer objects where possible; unsupported generated media is clearly labeled as a raster/video layer.

## 5. Time, frame and color invariants

- Store timeline positions in exact integer ticks or rational frame/sample coordinates. Avoid iterative floating-point seconds in timeline operations.
- Store source-media PTS/timebase separately from sequence/timeline time.
- Support variable-frame-rate sources using actual presentation timestamps; do not assume one constant frame interval for all source files.
- Audio sample positions use sample indices and sample rate; synchronize audio to video with explicit conversion rules.
- Display timecode is a formatted view, not a database key.
- Sequence settings explicitly contain width, height, pixel aspect ratio, frame rate, color space, transfer function, bit depth and audio sample rate.
- Preview proxy quality can differ from final render quality, but geometry/timing must be consistent and any quality differences declared.
- Color transforms must declare input/output spaces and order; never silently apply unknown LUTs.

## 6. Rendering and preview

Preview is a separate quality tier from final render. The system should support low-resolution interactive proxy preview, paused-frame high-quality preview, review render, and final render. Final render reads an immutable project revision. Render jobs return progress, logs with redacted values, per-segment cache metrics, exact render settings and an output manifest. Export cancellation must clean temporary work without deleting the project or source media.

## 7. Cloud deployment model — not yet chosen

Google Cloud is a plausible option because the user plans to use Google AI Studio, but vendor lock-in and cost should be compared before selection. Possible components include Cloud Run for API/control services, Cloud Run Jobs or a queue-backed worker for batch work, Firebase Auth, Firestore or SQL for project metadata, and Cloud Storage for media. Cloud Run GPU capability and quotas vary; GPU instances have minimum CPU/RAM shapes and are paid resources. Firebase Cloud Storage currently requires a Blaze billing account. No billing, card or deployment assumption is made.

## 8. Failure isolation and consistency

- A failed AI model must not corrupt the project document.
- A worker retry must be idempotent; use `job_id + operation_id` deduplication.
- A batch has `PREPARED → AWAITING_APPROVAL → APPLYING → VERIFYING → COMMITTED` or `FAILED/ROLLED_BACK` lifecycle.
- Conflicting comments must be surfaced; never silently choose last-write-wins for overlapping geometry/text operations.
- If project revision changed since comment capture, run anchor re-resolution and show a conflict report.
- If an external provider times out after receiving an expensive request, use provider task IDs/recovery where supported; otherwise require explicit user choice before retry.

## 9. Architecture decisions still OPEN

Cloud vendor/region; database; media store; worker runtime; queue; authentication; project retention; engine selection; provider gateway implementation; cost controls; external formats priority; preview transport; and cloud GPU policy.

## 10. Acceptance gates

`TEST-ARCH-001`: native Kotlin/Compose project successfully created in the actual AI Studio account.  
`TEST-ARCH-002`: Android client authenticates and calls a non-paid health endpoint.  
`TEST-ARCH-003`: resumable upload to private bucket works with authorization; storage billing approval is recorded.  
`TEST-ARCH-004`: worker can probe a sample video and report codec/size/duration/FPS/VFR/audio/color metadata.  
`TEST-ARCH-005`: canonical project revision increments atomically under concurrent edits.  
`TEST-ARCH-006`: API keys never appear in APK, network response to client, logs or crash reports.  
`TEST-ARCH-007`: render job can be cancelled and retried without duplicate project mutations.


## 11. Current Google/Firebase/Cloud Run facts checked 2026-11-26

- Google AI Studio Build mode's current documentation describes both web application builds and native Android builds using Kotlin + Jetpack Compose. It describes a browser-based Android emulator, physical-device installation using browser ADB, a Code view and GitHub import in Build mode. The exact controls and access are still account/session-dependent and must be confirmed inside the user's session before work begins. Source: https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/aistudio-build-mode
- The same documentation distinguishes the web Build runtime (React client + Node.js server runtime) from Android Build output (Kotlin/Compose native project). AIVX deliberately keeps its production backend separate from the Android app and does not assume that Android Build mode provides a deployable private video-processing server.
- Firebase Cloud Storage requires the Firebase project to be on the pay-as-you-go Blaze plan. Google states that no-cost usage may still be available within the plan, but Spark-plan projects do not have access to Cloud Storage buckets. Therefore a linked billing account is a prerequisite even when use is expected to remain within no-cost allowances. Source: https://firebase.google.com/docs/storage/faqs-storage-changes-announced-sept-2024
- Cloud Run GPU services are currently documented for NVIDIA L4 and RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell. L4 requires at least 4 CPU and 16 GiB RAM; full RTX PRO 6000 requires 20 CPU and 80 GiB RAM. GPU services require billing to be enabled, GPU quota, and instance-based billing configuration; the service can scale to zero when idle. GPU work is therefore not treated as free or automatically approved. Source: https://docs.cloud.google.com/run/docs/configuring/services/gpu

These facts are current-source evidence, not a commitment to use Google Cloud. Verify product availability, quotas, billing, region and cost again immediately before selecting a cloud architecture.
CANONICAL_PROJECT_MODEL.md — 115 lines
# AIVX — Canonical Editable Project Model

**Document ID:** AIVX-DATA-001 · **Version:** 1.0 · **Date:** 2026-11-26  
**Status:** PROPOSAL; no engine or DB schema is final.  
**Related:** FEAT-01..15, DATA-001..030, API-001..045, ADR-003, TEST-020..039

## 1. Source-of-truth rules

1. The server stores the canonical project document and operation history.
2. The Android client may cache a snapshot for responsiveness but cannot authoritatively commit a new revision locally.
3. AI workers may read an immutable snapshot and return a typed `EditPlan`; they may not directly patch raw project JSON.
4. All edits pass through the Project Command API, validate against current revision, are recorded, and are undoable.
5. Assets are immutable objects referenced by IDs. Replaced assets create new versions or derivative references; do not silently overwrite original source.
6. A render job pins `project_revision`, renderer/worker version, export settings and input asset versions.
7. Every time-based item carries source and timeline domains separately. Frame time is represented exactly at sequence boundaries.

## 2. Core entities

| Entity | Key fields | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Project | `projectId`, `ownerId`, `memberIds`, `schemaVersion`, `revision`, `settings`, `sequenceIds`, `assetIds` | One private editing workspace |
| Sequence | `sequenceId`, `projectId`, `frameRate`, `width`, `height`, `pixelAspect`, `colorConfig`, `audioSampleRate`, `trackIds`, `duration` | Timebase and output settings |
| Track | `trackId`, `sequenceId`, `kind`, `zOrder`, `visible`, `locked`, `muted`, `solo`, `blendMode`, `trackEffects` | A layer lane |
| Clip | `clipId`, `trackId`, `assetId`, `kind`, `timelineStart`, `timelineDuration`, `sourceIn`, `sourceOut`, `speed`, `transform`, `opacity`, `effects`, `keyframeTracks` | Clip instance, not source asset |
| Asset | `assetId`, `objectKey`, `sha256`, `mediaKind`, `probe`, `duration`, `streams`, `derivatives`, `provenance` | Immutable input/derived media |
| TextLayer | `textLayerId`, `clipId`, `text`, `fontRef`, `fontSize`, `fontWeight`, `tracking`, `leading`, `fill`, `stroke`, `shadow`, `alignment`, `animation` | Native editable text where supported |
| ShapeLayer | `shapeId`, `geometry`, `fill`, `stroke`, `transform`, `keyframes` | Shape/vector object |
| Effect | `effectId`, `effectType`, `params`, `enabled`, `maskIds`, `keyframeTracks`, `engineId`, `version` | Typed effect instance |
| Mask | `maskId`, `path`, `feather`, `expansion`, `opacity`, `inverted`, `mode`, `trackingBinding` | Path geometry and temporal data |
| KeyframeTrack | `targetId`, `propertyPath`, `interpolation`, `keyframes[]` | Per-property animation curve |
| Marker | `markerId`, `time`, `label`, `color`, `notes` | Sequence marker |
| Annotation | `annotationId`, `revision`, `sequenceId`, `timeAnchor`, `rangeAnchor`, `geometry`, `candidateTargets`, `commentId` | Exact visual feedback anchor |
| Comment | `commentId`, `text`, `status`, `priority`, `annotationId`, `createdBy`, `createdAt` | User intent tied to evidence |
| Operation | `operationId`, `commandType`, `targetIds`, `payload`, `baseRevision`, `resultRevision`, `inversePayload`, `actor`, `source` | Manual or AI operation audit record |
| EditPlan | `planId`, `basedOnRevision`, `operations[]`, `conflicts[]`, `confidence`, `costEstimate`, `evidenceRefs`, `approvalState` | AI proposal before applying |
| Job | `jobId`, `jobType`, `status`, `progress`, `inputRevision`, `workerVersion`, `costEstimate`, `actualCost`, `resultAssetIds`, `error` | Async operation lifecycle |

## 3. Time representation

Represent time internally with integer ticks or rational time. The canonical model must hold:
- `timelineTime`: sequence-relative timeline coordinate.
- `sourceTime`: media presentation coordinate (PTS/timebase) in the asset.
- `frameIndex`: integer sequence frame index where an operation is frame-bound.
- `sampleIndex`: integer audio sample index where audio edit is sample-bound.
- `frameRate`: numerator/denominator pair; do not store a floating-point FPS as the only value.
- `sourceTimebase`: media stream timebase numerator/denominator.

Conversion is explicit, tested and performed at system boundaries. Variable-frame-rate video uses timestamps from the source media. Display strings such as `00:00:02:15` are presentation-only. For a 29.97 drop-frame sequence, the timecode format is metadata and not a direct integer-seconds conversion.

FilmCraft uses a very fine fixed integer tick rate internally; this is one possible implementation. If adopted, AIVX must use a tested adapter and not assume its time ticks are identical to other sources.

## 4. Example project JSON envelope (illustrative, not an approved production schema)

```json
{
  "schemaVersion": 1,
  "projectId": "prj_example",
  "revision": 12,
  "sequenceIds": ["seq_main"],
  "assets": [],
  "sequences": [],
  "operationHead": "op_000012",
  "updatedAt": "2026-11-26T00:00:00Z"
}
```

Do not create production DB tables from this sketch without design approval and migration tests.

## 5. Command contract

All edits must use a command envelope:

```json
{
  "commandId": "cmd_opaque_id",
  "idempotencyKey": "job-or-user-generated-stable-key",
  "projectId": "prj_example",
  "baseRevision": 12,
  "commandType": "text.updateStyle",
  "targetIds": ["txt_3"],
  "payload": {"fontFamilyRef": "font_roboto", "fontWeight": 700},
  "actor": {"type": "user", "id": "usr_owner"},
  "source": {"type": "ai_batch", "planId": "plan_42", "commentIds": ["com_2"]}
}
```

Server response returns committed revision, operation ID, resulting target summaries and validation warnings. If `baseRevision` is stale, reject or run a conflict resolver; never silently overwrite.

## 6. Project schema evolution

- Every document has `schemaVersion`.
- Every migration has a deterministic unit test with old fixture and expected new fixture.
- Never edit a migration already shipped; add a new migration.
- Unsupported newer schemas are refused with clear message.
- Keep migrations reversible where feasible; when not reversible, document the loss and back up the original.
- Project export includes canonical JSON, media manifest, fonts/asset references, effect definitions/version IDs and any external resources needed.

## 7. Effect and renderer abstraction

An effect declares: stable ID; schema version; input/output domains; parameter schema/ranges/defaults; color-space requirements; whether it needs source frames, neighboring frames, audio, other tracks or a tracking model; preview quality; render resource class; alpha handling; GPU/CPU fallback; deterministic behavior; unsupported interchange behavior. The Project Service does not know vendor-specific worker implementation details.

## 8. Native editability categories

Every imported/generated object is assigned one of:
- `NATIVE_EDITABLE`: actual text/vector/shape/clip/layer/effect with parameters preserved.
- `RECONSTRUCTED_EDITABLE`: AI-derived approximation represented as layers but not source-identical.
- `RASTERIZED`: flattened video/image/object without underlying typography/geometry data.
- `GENERATED_MEDIA`: model output video/image/audio whose internal latent structure is not editable.
- `EXTERNAL_REFERENCE`: object stored in another editor/project format; not yet imported into canonical model.

UI must never describe a rasterized caption as editable text before OCR/reconstruction succeeds.

## 9. Retention and deletion

Project delete must schedule removal of source, proxies, previews, analysis files, embeddings, comments, export files and backups in accordance with the selected retention policy. A tombstone and deletion job track completion. Provider copies cannot always be purged by our system; disclose each provider’s retention controls and record user choice.
FEAT15_VISUAL_FEEDBACK.md — 121 lines
# FEAT-15 — Visual Feedback, Region Anchors and Batch AI Revision

**Document ID:** AIVX-FEAT15-001 · **Version:** 1.0 · **Date:** 2026-11-26  
**Status:** APPROVED PRODUCT REQUIREMENT; detailed interaction and backend design PROPOSAL  
**Priority:** P0/P1 differentiator  
**Related:** SCR-08, SCR-09, DATA-011..018, API-021..030, SEC-009..015, TEST-041..052, ADR-006

## 1. User problem

A user is watching a video in the preview and notices a small problem: for example, the third of three text items visible at 4.0 seconds uses the wrong font. The user should not need to take a screenshot, crop it, upload it, re-explain the time, identify the target or explain the project layers. They should mark the target directly on the current frame and write a short instruction. The system captures enough context automatically to resolve what the user meant.

The user may add many notes at different moments and then run all requested changes once. The comments are individual records, not one long prompt string that loses target identity.

## 2. Interaction model

The preview offers a small feedback tool palette:
- `POINT`: tap an element or point.
- `CIRCLE`: draw an ellipse/circle around an object.
- `LASSO`: freehand region polygon.
- `RECT`: rectangular region.
- `ARROW`: pointer annotation plus optional target region.
- `TIME_RANGE`: mark an interval on the timeline or by dragging across playback.
- `LAYER_PICK`: tap a visible editable object if the renderer exposes hit-test IDs.

The user can pause or select a frame; tool overlay must not change the rendered video. After drawing, a compact composer appears anchored to the marked region. User adds natural-language note, optional priority and save. The UI immediately adds a comment chip/card showing time, target hint and status. The user can continue playback and add more comments without leaving the preview.

## 3. Automatic evidence capture

For each annotation, capture:
1. `projectId`, `sequenceId`, `projectRevision`, `previewRenderId`, `rendererVersion`.
2. Sequence `frameIndex`, `timelineTime`, `frameRate` numerator/denominator and exact rational/tick form.
3. Source `assetId`, source PTS/timebase and source time if current frame maps to one source clip.
4. Stage geometry: original stage width/height, normalized polygon/ellipse/rectangle points, viewport-to-stage transform, preview scaling and letterbox/crop offsets.
5. Selected hit-test targets (clip/layer/text/effect/mask IDs) and their visible bounding boxes if the renderer exposes them.
6. A frame reference or secure server-side render reference; optional low-resolution crop derived on the server. Avoid duplicate full-resolution uploads from the Android phone.
7. Existing OCR/text boxes, object detections, mask/tracking IDs and transcript context when available.
8. User text instruction, locale, comment order, priority and capture timestamp.
9. A context summary that distinguishes observed metadata from AI-detected suggestions and uncertainty.

No image is silently transmitted to an external model. The system must disclose which provider receives the frame/crop and require consent under the project’s media policy.

## 4. Target resolution

Resolution order:
1. Exact renderer object hit-test ID if user tapped a native element.
2. Intersect selected normalized region with visible layer geometry at selected time.
3. Match text OCR boxes to editable text layers using text similarity, font/layout and temporal overlap.
4. Match object segmentation/tracking IDs to media layers and masks.
5. Ask VLM to describe candidates or distinguish visual targets only when needed.
6. If several candidates remain plausible, show a candidate picker; do not choose silently.

A target resolution result includes `targetType`, `targetId`, `candidateIds`, `confidence`, `evidenceRefs`, `editableState`, `timeSpan`, `geometry` and `resolutionMethod`. If no safe editable target exists, mark `UNRESOLVED` and explain why.

## 5. Raster vs editable content handling

- If target is a native text layer: update font family/weight/size, color, tracking, leading, stroke/shadow and keyframes as directed.
- If text has been flattened into video pixels: OCR can identify visible words/region, but cannot recover the original font file or text layer. The system may propose mask/inpaint to remove old text and create a new native text layer. Label this `RECONSTRUCTED_EDITABLE`, show seam/occlusion risk and require review.
- If the target is a generated video model clip: regenerate only the relevant segment if model supports it, or composite an editable overlay. Never claim the original generated clip’s internal elements are structurally editable.
- If font/asset unavailable: show fallback font or request a replacement selection. Do not silently substitute a near font while saying exact match.

## 6. Comment data and lifecycle

Statuses: `OPEN`, `RESOLVING`, `RESOLVED`, `NEEDS_CLARIFICATION`, `READY`, `IN_BATCH`, `APPLIED`, `VERIFIED`, `FAILED`, `CONFLICTED`, `IGNORED`.

Each comment is independently editable, movable on timeline, re-anchorable, duplicable and resolvable. Deleting a comment does not delete source media or project objects. Marking resolved is not the same as applying a change.

If the project changes after the annotation, preserve the original captured revision. Re-resolve by stable layer ID plus time/geometry. If the target moved or was deleted, show `CONFLICTED` and provide re-anchor options.

## 7. Batch Run pipeline

```mermaid
flowchart TD
 A[User presses Run] --> B[Freeze selected comment set]
 B --> C[Read project revision + all anchors]
 C --> D[Resolve targets and evidence]
 D --> E[Group compatible operations]
 E --> F[Detect overlap / dependencies / stale anchors]
 F --> G[Build typed EditPlan]
 G --> H[Dry-run render + cost estimate]
 H --> I[Show diff, risks and unresolved items]
 I --> J{User approves?}
 J -- No --> K[Return to comments; no project edits]
 J -- Yes --> L[Recheck revision and permissions]
 L --> M[Apply one atomic undoable batch]
 M --> N[Render changed time ranges]
 N --> O[Visual/QC verification]
 O --> P[Per-comment PASS / FAIL / REVIEW]
```

### 7.1 Planning rules

- Freeze comment IDs/order and project revision for one batch.
- Group edits only when they target same compatible object/property/time span.
- If comments conflict (e.g. change text color to red and later blue), show a conflict and ask which wins or apply explicit order if the user specified order.
- If edits depend on one another, compute a dependency graph and show execution sequence.
- If an AI proposal touches objects outside marked time/range/target, flag it as scope expansion and require separate approval.
- Generate a dry-run summary containing operation list, before/after preview, target/time, confidence, external service, estimated/actual cost, destructive/inpaint/generation step, and rollback availability.
- No spend-incurring external request until the approval policy is satisfied.

### 7.2 Atomicity and rollback

All approved commands execute within one batch transaction. If a command fails before commit, no partial project revision becomes visible. If rendering/QC fails after the project mutation committed, keep the project change, report the render/QC failure, and offer undo; do not automatically discard a valid project edit because a preview worker crashed. A user-visible `Undo Batch` must restore the exact previous project revision.

## 8. Example

Project revision 23; sequence 30fps; the user circles a text line at 4.0s and comments “change this font”. The anchor resolves to `textLayerId=txt_03`, native editable, confidence 0.99. At 5.0s they mark background and comment “make the background darker”; target resolves to `clip_04` background layer, confidence 0.93. At 8.2s they mark a flattened logo and comment “replace logo”; OCR/vision classifies raster-only, so planner proposes inpaint + editable SVG overlay, confidence 0.72, risk medium. Run produces three operations in one plan. The user can approve all or exclude the logo. Execution reports per-comment status and a batch undo operation.

## 9. Acceptance tests

- TEST-041: Circle on known native text layer at a fixed frame resolves to correct layer ID and time within one sequence frame.
- TEST-042: Rectangle/lasso geometry remains correct under preview scaling, letterboxing, rotation and device DPI.
- TEST-043: User can add at least 20 independent comments without screenshot upload or losing captured timestamps.
- TEST-044: Comment context stores project revision and asset/layer IDs, not only natural-language text.
- TEST-045: A text-target comment changes only the selected text layer and keeps its track/timing intact.
- TEST-046: Raster-only text is not misrepresented as editable text; it offers reconstructed-layer workflow with warning.
- TEST-047: Conflicting comments are surfaced before approval.
- TEST-048: User declines plan; no project revision or provider job is created.
- TEST-049: Approved batch applies as one undoable operation group.
- TEST-050: Worker failure during pre-commit causes no partial project mutation.
- TEST-051: Project revision changes between annotation and Run; stale anchors are revalidated and conflicts displayed.
- TEST-052: Post-batch visual comparison inspects the marked time/range and returns evidence-backed verification.
PROVIDER_GATEWAY.md — 52 lines
# BYOK Provider Gateway and AI Capability Router

**Document ID:** AIVX-API-001 · **Version:** 1.0 · **Status:** PROPOSAL  
**Related:** FEAT-13/14/15, API-031..045, SEC-001..008, TEST-060..069

## 1. Objective

Allow the owner to configure their own AI service credentials and endpoints without tying the app to one model vendor. Credentials are stored and used on the server only. Android sends a provider profile ID and task request, never a secret.

## 2. Provider profile

`ProviderProfile` fields: `profileId`, `ownerId`, `displayName`, `protocolFamily`, `baseUrl`, `endpointOverrides`, `authType`, `secretRef`, `customHeaders` (non-secret values only), `defaultModel`, `requestAdapterId`, `responseAdapterId`, `capabilities`, `limits`, `pricingConfig`, `region`, `timeoutPolicy`, `retryPolicy`, `enabled`, `priority`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`, `lastTestResult`.

Secret values are separately stored in a secrets vault and represented by `secretRef`. They are encrypted at rest with a managed key service or equivalent. Secret read is prohibited for ordinary database/report endpoints. Secret rotation and deletion are explicit operations.

## 3. Protocol families

- OpenAI-compatible chat/completions or responses (capability-specific; do not assume full API parity).
- Anthropic Messages API style.
- Google Gemini API or OpenAI-compatible endpoint where explicitly supported by vendor.
- Custom HTTP JSON adapter with user-mapped request/response paths, auth header/query field, streaming mode, image/audio/video upload method and error mapping.
- Provider-specific official SDK adapter for features that cannot be expressed by generic JSON mapping.

A service is marked `COMPATIBLE` only after a successful capability test. A base URL alone is not enough.

## 4. Capability manifest

Capabilities are explicit booleans/values: `textInput`, `imageInput`, `videoInput`, `audioInput`, `structuredOutput`, `toolCalling`, `streaming`, `jsonMode`, `longContextTokens`, `maxImages`, `maxVideoDuration`, `maxFileBytes`, `maxOutputTokens`, `supportsTimestamps`, `supportsVideoFrames`, `supportsAudioTranscription`, `supportsBatch`, `supportsTaskPolling`, `region`, `dataRetentionDeclared`, `knownPricing`.

A task router checks capability requirements before it selects the provider. If the profile lacks video input, the orchestrator may extract frames/audio and send those modalities only if the user has allowed that provider to receive them.

## 5. Connection tests

Non-destructive test sequence: validate URL scheme/host; DNS/TLS; confirm no private IP/loopback/link-local/metadata endpoint (SSRF controls); send minimal authenticated text request; optionally test structured output and one small user-authorized media sample; parse response; record latency/error category/model identity/capabilities. The test must not print the secret.

## 6. Security controls

- Do not allow arbitrary user-supplied URL fetching by the Android app backend. Provider endpoints require SSRF protections, domain/IP policy and egress controls.
- Never place secrets in client config, generated Build files, logs, analytics, screenshots, test fixtures or crash diagnostics.
- Redact headers, query strings and provider error bodies before storage/return.
- Limit custom headers and forbid overriding reserved transport/security headers.
- Rate-limit per user/profile; set maximum request/response bytes and timeouts.
- Enforce task-level modality consent and media retention disclosure.
- Store provider task IDs for recovery; do not blindly retry requests that may have billed after timeout.

## 7. Fallback and model routing

Priority is user configuration. Fallback is allowed only when the destination provider has the required modality/capability and the user has permitted that media to be transferred there. No silent cross-provider fallback with private media. Present provider name, destination region and expected cost before first upload to a new provider.

## 8. Tests

TEST-060 secret never appears in Android traffic response or project export. TEST-061 provider key is redacted from logs/errors. TEST-062 mismatched capability blocks task before file upload. TEST-063 unknown custom response schema is marked unsupported, not silently guessed. TEST-064 retry after ambiguous timeout does not duplicate billed task without recovery check. TEST-065 disabled profile cannot be selected. TEST-066 SSRF/private IP target blocked. TEST-067 owner A cannot read owner B provider profile/secret. TEST-068 provider deletion clears vault reference and scheduled metadata. TEST-069 cost limit blocks new jobs and returns clear reason.
AI_AGENT_EDIT_PROTOCOL.md — 97 lines
# AI Agent Editing Protocol — Inspect, Plan, Approve, Apply, Verify

**Document ID:** AIVX-API-002 · **Version:** 1.0 · **Status:** PROPOSAL  
**Related:** FEAT-13/14/15, API-020..030, DATA-018..024, SEC-009..020, TEST-041..059

## 1. Core rule

The agent is a planner/operator that invokes typed project tools. It is not a free-form code executor with unrestricted access to storage or the project database. All changes are generated as structured operations and committed by the Project Service. The user remains in control of the approved edit scope.

## 2. Tool capability tiers

### Read-only tools

`project.inspect`, `project.getRevision`, `sequence.inspect`, `timeline.getRange`, `layer.inspect`, `asset.inspect`, `media.probe`, `media.getProxy`, `frame.get`, `frame.crop`, `annotation.list`, `comment.list`, `transcript.get`, `ocr.get`, `scene.list`, `analysis.report`, `effects.catalog`, `fonts.catalog`, `providers.capabilities`, `jobs.list`, `export.inspectSupport`.

### Proposal tools

`analysis.createPlan`, `feedback.resolveTargets`, `edit.planFromPrompt`, `edit.planFromComments`, `edit.validatePlan`, `edit.estimateCost`, `edit.previewPlan`, `export.plan`.

### Mutation tools (approval-gated)

`project.command.apply`, `project.batch.apply`, `project.undo`, `project.redo`, `media.asset.attach`, `export.start`, `generation.start`, `worker.start`.

### Restricted/destructive tools

`project.delete`, `asset.delete`, `provider.delete`, `comment.purge`, `project.restore` require separate confirmation and strict permission checks. These are never available implicitly to the AI planning loop.

## 3. Standard operation envelope

```json
{
  "operationId": "op_01H...",
  "commandType": "text.updateStyle",
  "targetIds": ["txt_03"],
  "timeRange": {"sequenceStart": "120/30", "sequenceEnd": "150/30"},
  "payload": {"fontFamilyRef": "font_inter", "fontWeight": 700},
  "preconditions": {"targetRevision": 5, "expectedTextHash": "sha256:..."},
  "evidenceRefs": ["ann_42", "frame_4_0"],
  "confidence": 0.96,
  "risk": "LOW",
  "estimatedCost": {"currency": "USD", "amount": 0.00}
}
```

The JSON schema is a design sketch. Production schemas must be versioned and validated at the server boundary. `timeRange` should use canonical time units/rationals, not lossy strings; the example is illustrative.

## 4. Required plan lifecycle

`DRAFT → VALIDATING → READY_FOR_REVIEW → APPROVED → APPLYING → VERIFYING → COMMITTED`.
Alternative terminal states: `REJECTED`, `CANCELLED`, `CONFLICTED`, `FAILED`, `ROLLED_BACK`, `NEEDS_CLARIFICATION`.

A plan record captures: user prompt; source comments; project revision; context references; resolved targets; planned operations; tool versions; provider/model route; costs; uncertainty; conflicts; unsupported features; risk; preview render references; approval identity/time; execution result; verification checks; inverse/undo group.

## 5. Safe edit loop

1. Inspect only relevant project context and user-provided assets.
2. Parse natural-language intent into target/property/time/action slots.
3. Resolve targets from IDs and visual anchors; request clarification if ambiguity affects the result.
4. Build operation plan and dependencies.
5. Validate ranges/schema/capabilities/permissions and detect unsupported features.
6. Run dry-run previews and estimates; no irreversible mutation yet.
7. Present concise summary and detailed expandable diff.
8. Receive explicit approval for the plan or selected subset.
9. Re-check base revision and permissions immediately before mutation.
10. Apply atomic batch; store operation history.
11. Render the affected interval; compare with expected target and run QC.
12. Return a result for every user comment and an undo action.

## 6. Conflict rules

- Two comments modify the same property at overlapping time spans: mark `CONFLICTED`; do not guess.
- A comment refers to a removed/moved layer: attempt re-resolution with IDs + geometry + time; ask user if confidence below threshold.
- Two operations are compatible and target different layers: may batch.
- A proposed change affects unmarked parts of the film: show scope expansion and ask permission.
- Project revision advanced after plan creation: revalidate all preconditions and create a new plan revision if changed.

## 7. Evidence and explanation

AI can show a short decision summary: “I found the selected region at 00:04.00–00:04.83 and matched it to Text Layer 3 (confidence 0.96). It is a native text layer, so font can be changed directly.” It must not expose hidden chain-of-thought. The user receives evidence, target, confidence, affected time, operation summary, limitations and rollback path.

## 8. Agent tool permissions

Every call includes user ID, project role, project ID, job ID if applicable, allowed resource scope and operation class. Read-only and mutation permissions are different. A tool call is rejected if its declared permission exceeds the user role or the current approval state. Workers receive scoped object references, not broad bucket credentials.

## 9. Required security constraints

- Never execute arbitrary shell commands derived from a prompt.
- Never accept absolute filesystem paths from a client as trusted worker paths.
- Never allow external tools to write raw canonical project files.
- Never put provider keys or signed URLs in prompts, tool traces or model responses.
- Treat retrieved files and model output as untrusted input.
- Validate file type/content, decode safely, limit decompressed size and processing time.
- Keep user confirmation for paid provider calls, video generation, inpainting, large render and destructive operations.

## 10. Acceptance tests

TEST-053 invalid command type rejected; TEST-054 operation against stale revision rejected; TEST-055 agent cannot access a different project by guessing ID; TEST-056 read-only agent turn does not create project revision; TEST-057 approval required before mutation; TEST-058 tool permission revocation takes effect immediately; TEST-059 failed batch restores previous project revision and produces failure receipt.
EXPORT_INTERCHANGE.md — 61 lines
# Export, Interchange and Editable Project Compatibility

**Document ID:** AIVX-EXP-001 · **Version:** 1.0 · **Status:** PROPOSAL  
**Related:** FEAT-12, DATA-001..024, API-040..045, TEST-070..080, RISK-016

## 1. Two different outputs

1. **Rendered media:** MP4/MOV/WebM/GIF/image sequence/audio outputs. These are flattened deliverables. They do not preserve the entire editable project.
2. **Editable project/interchange:** track/clip order, trims, timing, media references, transforms, text, keyframes, effects, masks, transitions and metadata. This is a different export with potential feature loss.

The export screen must identify which kind is being generated.

## 2. Native AIVX project package

A project archive should include: versioned canonical project JSON; assets manifest; proxy/thumbnail manifest; supported generated source files (e.g. SVG/HTML/3D scene file) where needed; user fonts/links only when allowed; effect/plugin IDs and versions; timeline metadata; audio metadata; and a compatibility report. Private source media can be externalized or included based on user choice. All package paths must be relative and validated against path traversal.

## 3. Common interchange candidates

- OpenTimelineIO `.otio`/`.otioz`: editorial timeline/cut data and references; not a media container and not a complete representation of every compositor/effect.
- Final Cut Pro XML / FCPXML: timeline interchange with host-specific capabilities and version differences.
- CMX 3600 EDL: simple cut lists; very limited layers/effects/audio/animation.
- AAF/OMF: professional interchange for editorial/audio workflows; media essence, plugin and effect mapping varies.
- Premiere XML/AAF/FCPXML imports and DaVinci Resolve timeline interchange must be validated on exact app version.
- Subtitle formats: SRT/VTT/ASS/SCC as appropriate; subtitle styling/animation can be lost when flattened to simple text timing.

FilmCraft's interchange documentation claims EDL, Final Cut Pro 7 XML, FCPXML, OTIO, AAF, OMF and ALE import/export. Its own module documents unsupported concepts and report warnings. This makes it an important candidate, not proof of full fidelity to every destination editor.

## 4. App-specific caution

- CapCut has multiple regional/mobile/desktop formats and changing import behavior. No universal third-party project compatibility should be claimed without tests on the actual app/version/device.
- Alight Motion's project-package workflow is different from ordinary XML/OTIO; validate its documented current import/share process before designing an adapter.
- Node Video uses its own project/template workflows; do not assume open timeline interchange.
- Premiere Pro and DaVinci Resolve support professional interchange pathways, but the set of preserved effects, fonts, media relinks, nested sequences, keyframes and color transforms still varies.

## 5. Export matrix

| Output | Initial support proposal | Required verification |
|---|---|---|
| MP4 H.264 + AAC | P0 | Android playback, audio sync, duration, frame rate, audio peak, decoder compatibility |
| MOV ProRes | Optional | worker build, decoder, color/alpha handling, target editor open |
| WebM VP9/AV1 | Optional | device playback and alpha if supported |
| GIF | Optional | color palette/quality/file size |
| PNG/JPEG frame sequence | Optional | frame count, order, timestamps, alpha |
| WAV/FLAC/MP3/AAC | Optional | sample rate, channel layout, loudness/peak |
| SRT/VTT/ASS | P1 | text, word timings, style differences |
| OTIO | P2 | read/write, media relink, round-trip and loss report |
| FCPXML/EDL/AAF/OMF | P2 | destination editor import; feature parity report |
| CapCut/Alight Motion/Node Video native project | DEFERRED | exact target format, app version and round-trip tests |

## 6. Feature-loss report

Every interchange export should list:
- Preserved: clip timing, source trim, tracks, supported transforms, supported text/audio items.
- Approximation: font substitution, effects converted to baked video, speed curves approximated, flattening.
- Dropped: unsupported 3D camera, plugin effect, nested composition, advanced mask, proprietary generation metadata.
- Required media: missing files, linked assets, proxy/original references.
- Target app version and adapter version used.

## 7. Acceptance tests

TEST-070 rendered MP4 probe and playback pass; TEST-071 A/V sync within agreed tolerance; TEST-072 alpha verified with checkerboard; TEST-073 30/60/120fps frame count verified; TEST-074 OTIO import/export round-trip preserves supported clips; TEST-075 unsupported effects appear in loss report; TEST-076 Premiere import test; TEST-077 Resolve import test; TEST-078 CapCut import test on selected app version; TEST-079 Alight Motion package import test; TEST-080 Node Video project import test. No target passes before the actual import is verified.
SECURITY_THREAT_MODEL.md — 92 lines
# Security, Privacy and Abuse-Resistance Threat Model

**Document ID:** AIVX-SEC-001 · **Version:** 1.0 · **Status:** PROPOSAL / REQUIRED BEFORE CLOUD RELEASE  
**Related:** SEC-001..030, NFR-001..015, DATA-001..030, RISK-001..040

## 1. Assets to protect

Source videos/photos/audio; generated media; comments and annotations; project structure; transcripts and OCR; user identities and friend access; BYOK provider secrets; signed storage URLs; cloud worker credentials; model prompts/results; billing identifiers and job usage; logs and backups.

## 2. Trust boundaries

1. Android device ↔ API gateway.
2. API gateway ↔ identity/auth service.
3. API ↔ project DB and object storage.
4. Orchestrator ↔ external provider APIs.
5. Queue ↔ untrusted job input and isolated workers.
6. Worker ↔ object storage and rendered output.
7. Plugin/skill/third-party executable ↔ project data.
8. Export package ↔ target application/user file system.

## 3. Threats and controls

### Credential theft / provider secret exposure
Threat: keys accidentally enter APK, client logs, prompts, crash reports or public object metadata. Controls: server vault, secret references, KMS envelope encryption, strict redaction, no client retrieval endpoint, key rotation, per-provider access scope, test with APK/static artifact scanning.

### Cross-project data exposure
Threat: user guesses another project/asset/job ID. Controls: server-side ownership/role authorization on every API and object read; non-sequential opaque IDs do not replace authorization; object keys must be tenant-scoped; negative tests across two accounts.

### Malicious uploads / parser vulnerabilities
Threat: malformed media, decompression bombs, codec/parser exploit, huge dimensions, pathological duration, oversized manifests. Controls: quarantine upload, type sniffing, malware/file checks where available, resource caps, sandboxed decode workers, patched dependencies, no privileged service account inside worker, per-job timeout and memory limit.

### SSRF / provider endpoint abuse
Threat: user-configured endpoint hits cloud metadata, internal IP, loopback or private network. Controls: scheme allowlist; resolve/recheck DNS; block private/reserved/link-local/metadata ranges; egress proxy; redirect validation; request/response size limits; TLS policy; no access to internal service endpoints.

### Prompt injection / untrusted media instructions
Threat: on-screen text/audio/metadata tells the AI to exfiltrate data or perform unapproved operations. Controls: treat media/OCR/transcript/document content as untrusted evidence, never as system instructions; tool allowlists; project-scoped data access; no arbitrary code execution; approval gates.

### Destructive or unintended AI edits
Threat: wrong target, scope expansion, accidental deletion or conflicting comments. Controls: typed EditPlan, confidence, dry-run, affected-time list, atomic transactions, undo, stale revision detection, explicit approval for destructive operations.

### Cost abuse / repeated generation
Threat: runaway GPU loops, provider retries, large uploads, repeated expensive exports. Controls: user/project/job quota, budget ceiling, rate limits, idempotency keys, bounded retries, cost estimates, circuit breakers, admin/user alerts.

### Signed URL leakage
Threat: private media URL leaked in logs, screenshots or chat. Controls: short TTL, single object/action scope, no logging query string, use backend proxy where required, revoke ability where store supports it.

### Friend permission abuse
Threat: commenter changes timeline or sees keys. Controls: least privilege; COMMENTER can comment but cannot execute edits unless assigned EDITOR; no provider secret access for friends unless explicit account ownership transfer model is approved.

### Backup and deletion gaps
Threat: project delete leaves derived files/backups/provider retained copies. Controls: data inventory, object tags/manifest, deletion job receipt, backup expiry policy, provider-specific retention notice, restoration/deletion tests.

## 4. Security requirements

- SEC-001: TLS for all network transport.
- SEC-002: authentication required for private resources.
- SEC-003: object-level authorization enforced by backend.
- SEC-004: BYOK keys never delivered to Android after save.
- SEC-005: logs and error reports redact secrets and signed URLs.
- SEC-006: storage objects private by default.
- SEC-007: upload size, duration, resolution and decode resource limits.
- SEC-008: isolated worker process/container, least privilege and ephemeral workspace.
- SEC-009: AI operations use typed schema and allowlisted tools.
- SEC-010: external-provider upload requires explicit media-sharing consent.
- SEC-011: per-user/job cost and rate limits.
- SEC-012: idempotency for external and internal jobs.
- SEC-013: every edit carries authenticated actor and audit record.
- SEC-014: batch edit has approval and rollback path.
- SEC-015: project schema migrations are backed up and tested.
- SEC-016: secure deletion/retention policy.
- SEC-017: secrets rotation and revocation.
- SEC-018: no arbitrary shell execution from user prompt.
- SEC-019: prevent SSRF and path traversal.
- SEC-020: plugin/workflow execution is sandboxed and permission-scoped.
- SEC-021: dependency and container images are pinned/scanned.
- SEC-022: no service-account credentials in source repository, Android app or logs.
- SEC-023: incident response and credential revocation process documented.
- SEC-024: Android network security config avoids cleartext traffic.
- SEC-025: crash analytics do not capture frame images, raw transcripts or keys.
- SEC-026: friend roles are tested with a deny-by-default permission matrix.
- SEC-027: export/import packages are checked for zip-slip, path traversal and unsafe XML/JSON expansion.
- SEC-028: model output is validated and treated as untrusted.
- SEC-029: moderation/safety policies are applied to user media and generated content as required by chosen provider.
- SEC-030: backup restore and deletion test evidence stored before release.

## 5. Privacy notice content required

Before upload or external AI call, show: what data is sent (full video, extracted frames, audio, transcript, selected region), which service receives it, region if known, purpose, retention information from that provider, estimated job cost, and how to cancel/delete. Do not claim provider-side deletion unless provider API confirms it.

## 6. Tests

Security QA must include: two-account object isolation; role downgrade; expired URL; revoked provider key; SSRF private IP; file traversal; malformed media; oversize upload; prompt injection in on-screen text; repeated job requests; timeout after provider accepted task; batch conflict; deletion of all derivatives; log scanning for secrets; APK scan for secrets/native libraries; restore from backup; friend invite revocation.
